Termux doesn't come with GNU autotools (like `autoreconf`) pre-installed, and some libraries or Python packages expect a full GNU environment. ### **Solution Steps:** 1. **Install `autoconf` and related build tools in Termux:** ```bash pkg install autoconf automake libtool ``` 2. **Also make sure these are installed:** ```bash pkg install clang make cmake binutils ``` --- ### **Additional Tip:** If the build process still fails after these installs, and if **`patchelf`** is not crucial for your current Python package (often it’s not strictly required unless you're packaging binaries yourself), you may be able to **bypass or skip installing the dependency** by using: ```bash pip install --no-binary :all: [package-name] ``` Or for packages like `transformers`: ```bash pip install transformers --no-binary :all: ``` --- ### **Why This Happens in Termux:** - Some Python packages are trying to build **wheels from source** (especially if no pre-built wheels are available for ARM64 Android). - **`patchelf`** gets involved when the package uses compiled C extensions or bundled binaries (less common for pure Python libraries). --- If you tell me which package triggered the `patchelf` issue, I can suggest whether it's critical or if we can safely bypass it.

Every shard can carry its own history, verification details, tags, and children (nested shards).

Think: “Lego bricks for information,” with built-in versioning and provenance. ## Purpose - Make complex information **composable** out of small, consistent parts. - Track **what changed, when, and why** (version logs). - Capture **how trustworthy** a piece of data is (verification details + sources). - Let you **nest** shards to model documents, records, workflows, or cases in a clean tree. ## Features - **Shard creation & editing** Create root shards, define type/name, set atomicity, attach data (text/JSON/etc.). - **Nested structures** Add child shards to build trees (e.g., Case → Evidence → Notes). - **Version history** Each meaningful update produces a structured version entry (`versionId`, author, summary, data snapshot, timestamp). - **Verification details** Track verification status/method, sources (URLs, docs, user attestations), and timestamps. - **Explorer & detail views** Browse the shard tree, select a shard, edit its data/metadata, add children, or delete. - **Path-based operations** Utilities to `find`, `update`, `add`, and `delete` shards by path like `rootId/childId/grandchildId`. - **Optional AI assist** (Gemini) If you provide a `GEMINI_API_KEY`, some editing/generation helpers can be enabled. ## Major components (from `src/components/`) - `SystemConsoleDisplay` — boot/status messages, banner, principles. - `RootShardCreatorDisplay` — guided form to create root shards. - `ShardComplexExplorerDisplay` — tree/explorer for nested shards, select/delete. - `ShardDetailEditorDisplay` — edit data, versioning actions, verification info. - (Utility UI) `ShatterCoreConsole`, `SystemConsoleDisplay` and global styles. ## Core types & utilities - **Types** (`src/types/index.ts`, shard section) - `Shard`, `RootShardComplex`, `VersionEntry`, `VerificationDetails`, `ShardMetadata`, etc. - **Utilities** (`src/utils/shardUtils.ts`) - `findShardByPath`, `updateShardByPath`, `addNestedShardByPath`, `deleteShardByPath`, `createVersion(...)`. - **Constants** Present as `src/constants/shatterCoreProtocols.ts` (exports versions, banner, default shard types, core principles). > Rename this file to `src/constants/shardSystemConfig.ts` (see separation checklist) to match imports. ## Libraries - React 19, React DOM 19 - `@google/genai` (optional AI) - `react-hot-toast` (toasts) - `date-fns` (timestamps/formatting) - `clsx` (classnames) ## Known fixes (to keep ShatterCore standalone) - **Rename/match imports:** `src/ModularShardSystem.tsx` imports `./constants/shardSystemConfig`.

Finding or updating a small part risks breaking other parts. - **ShatterCore solution:** Each meaningful piece becomes a **shard** with its own type (e.g., `text_document`, `structured_json_object`, `evidence_item_meta`) and its own data.

You can grab just the piece you need, work on it, and put it back without touching unrelated content. **Why this helps processing:** Scripts and tools can operate on a shard type (e.g., “run this transformation on all `meeting_notes_segment` shards”) without guessing where that data lives inside big files. ### 2) Everything is nestable, so structure mirrors how you think - Build **trees**: a root shard (e.g., “Case Alpha”) contains children (evidence, notes, timelines), which contain their own children. - You get **human-friendly organization** and **machine-friendly structure** at the same time. **Why this helps processing:** Tree structures are easy to traverse.

Tools can walk the tree, apply rules per type, and aggregate results in predictable ways. ### 3) Every change is versioned with context - Shards keep a **version log**: who changed what, when, and a quick summary.

You reduce risk and improve repeatability. ### 4) Verification is built in (trust, sources, status) - Each shard version carries **verification details**: status (e.g., `unverified`, `user_verified`), method, list of **sources** (URLs, documents, user attestations), plus timestamps. - You can require “N sources” before you mark a shard as “ready.” **Why this helps processing:** Downstream tasks can **filter by trust level**.

Automation doesn’t need to scan huge blobs—just go straight to `case/evidence/12345`. ### 6) Rich metadata (tags, links, custom fields) - Attach **tags**, link shards with labeled relationships, and keep **custom properties** per shard. - Add structured **processing logs** to record how the data was used. **Why this helps processing:** You can build truly **smart filters** (“all shards tagged `timeline` verified after Sept 1”) and maintain **data lineage** (“this chart came from these five shards”). ### 7) Reuse without duplication - A shard can be **referenced or linked** in multiple places (e.g., the same user profile segment supports two different analyses). - Avoid copy/paste drift and keep **one source of truth**. **Why this helps processing:** Consistency.

Fix a shard once; every place that uses it benefits. ### 8) Easier collaboration and review - Each shard’s history and verification details are local to that shard.

Two teammates can safely work on different shards at the same time. - Reviewers can comment/verify just the affected shard, not an entire file. **Why this helps processing:** You move faster and break fewer things.

Create a new shard type and start using it. - No need to redesign a mega-schema or rewrite half your code. **Why this helps processing:** **Extensibility.** Your system adapts as your real-world needs change. --- ## TL;DR benefits for teams & pipelines - **Speed:** operate on small, typed chunks; avoid rescanning whole documents. - **Quality:** built-in versioning & verification improve trust and auditability. - **Depth:** nested structures + per-shard metadata enable sophisticated analysis. - **Safety:** localize changes and rollbacks to the affected shard only. - **Automation-ready:** path-based operations and types make scripting straightforward. - **Future-proof:** add new shard types without breaking old work. --- If you want, I can generate two **ready-to-commit** app folders (ShatterCore + Nudge) with corrected imports, minimal `package.json`s, and README files. > **User (2025-10-02 03:48:39 UTC):** > > Shattercore and nudge should be two separate apps completely.

That design changes what’s possible: ### 1) You edit safely, without breaking unrelated stuff **Before:** one big file; changing a paragraph risks messing up the rest. **With shards:** you edit just the **one piece** you care about.

Automations can jump straight to the exact shard—no scanning or grep-fests. ### 3) You always know what changed and why Each shard keeps a **version log**: who changed it, when, a quick summary, and a **snapshot** of the data at that moment.

If something goes wrong, you can **revert just that shard**.

Pipelines can **filter by trust level**—e.g., only use `source_verified` shards for reports and flag the rest for review. ### 5) You can organize like a human, process like a machine Shards **nest** into trees, so your structure matches the real world: Case → Evidence → Notes → Action Items.

Computers love trees—scripts can traverse them and apply rules per shard type. ### 6) You reuse facts without copy-paste chaos The same shard can be **referenced in multiple places**.

Reviewers can approve changes **shard-by-shard** with clear context. ### 10) You adapt without heavy migrations New data shape?

Create a **new shard type** and start using it—no enormous schema rewrite. --- ## Concrete examples - **Research project:** Store each source as a shard with citation metadata, notes as child shards, and claims as separate typed shards.

Automation runs only on `result_summary` shards with a matching `experiment_config` version and tags the outcome as `system_verified` once checks pass. --- ## The bottom line ShatterCore makes data **modular, traceable, verifiable, and automatable**.

You get: - **Speed** (operate on small, typed chunks) - **Quality** (versioning + verification) - **Depth** (nested structure + rich metadata) - **Safety** (revert a shard, not the world) - **Maintainability** (targeted automation + easy extensibility) If you want, I can draft the two app folders (file trees + fixed imports + minimal `package.json`s) so you can drop them straight into a workspace. > **User (2025-10-02 03:53:30 UTC):** > > How is this version of nudge different than the one previously uploaded? Hashing and text detection def sha256_file(p: Path) -> str: h = hashlib.sha256() with open(p, 'rb') as f: for chunk in iter(lambda: f.read(65536), b''): h.update(chunk) return h.hexdigest() def read_text(p: Path) -> Tuple[str, bool]: data = None try: data = p.read_text(encoding='utf-8') return data, True except UnicodeDecodeError: try: data = p.read_text(encoding='utf-8', errors='replace') return data, True except Exception: return "", False # Compare file sets prev_keys = set(prev_map.keys()) new_keys = set(new_map.keys()) added = sorted(list(new_keys - prev_keys)) removed = sorted(list(prev_keys - new_keys)) common = sorted(list(prev_keys & new_keys)) rows = [] def line_change_stats(old_text: str, new_text: str) -> Tuple[int,int,int]: # return (added, removed, changed-ish) diff = list(difflib.ndiff(old_text.splitlines(), new_text.splitlines())) adds = sum(1 for d in diff if d.startswith('+ ')) subs = sum(1 for d in diff if d.startswith('- ')) # It’s written as system/developer instructions and includes data models, flows, UI spec, and concrete outputs the model should produce. --- # Prompt for Google AI Studio — Build “Nudge: Pathways & Guiding Echoes” You are an expert full-stack engineer and product designer.

Build a modern, production-ready **web app** called **Nudge** that lets users design and run **structured, multi-step workflows** (“**Pathways**”) guided by configurable **personas** (“**Guiding Echoes**”) and driven by **DeltaShift** protocols (phased iteration).

The app should work entirely client-side for the MVP, with optional hooks for persistence. ## High-level concept (what Nudge is) Nudge helps people **iterate** on work (design, writing, coding, analysis) using reusable **curricula** (step lists).

Gemini should generate/refine content at each step when asked, using the **active Echo(es)**, selected **strategy/scale**, and the current **step’s instructions**. --- ## Goals - Easy **configuration** of Pathways with personas, link mode (how personas combine), strategy/scale, and curriculum. - Clear **execution** of steps with “advance / revert” controls and **trace logs**. - **Snapshots** of intermediate outputs so users can compare/checkpoint progress. - Clean, typed data **models** and a UI that feels modern, fast, and reliable. ## Non-goals (MVP) - No backend is required (provide interfaces and a persistence adapter so adding storage later is easy). - No multi-tenant auth (optional stub). --- ## Tech & quality bar - **Frontend:** React + TypeScript + Vite (or Next.js App Router if you prefer), minimal dependencies, modular architecture. - **LLM:** Google Generative AI SDK (Gemini).

A11y-friendly, keyboard navigable. - **UX polish:** Toasts for success/error, loading states, optimistic updates where safe, responsive layout. - **Testing:** Lightweight tests for core utilities (types/serializers/validators). - **Documentation:** In-app “Help” card + a concise README. --- ## Core concepts & data models (TypeScript) ```ts // Personas (Guiding Echoes) export interface EchoBit id: string; // prompt body for this step guidingEchoBitId?: string; // override persona per step (optional) outputNameTemplate?: string;// e.g., "Draft v n - title " export interface IterationCurriculum id: string; Such measures, critics note, essentially punish people *for their homelessness itself* – creating an “impossible choice,” as Justice Sonia Sotomayor wrote in dissent, to “either stay awake or be arrested”【10†L111-L115】. ## Criminalizing Survival and Eliminating Due Process Many of these laws and policies contain elements that **short-circuit due process protections** or broadly criminalize the status of being unhoused.

These provisions create powerful incentives for officials to remove homeless people from sight quickly, often without providing adequate notice or storage of personal property. **Encampment “sweeps”** – the mass clearing of homeless camps by police and sanitation crews – epitomize the due process concerns.

The similarity of new anti-homeless laws across numerous states is not coincidental – many are based on **model legislation** circulated by advocacy groups and think tanks.

For example, Missouri’s 2022 camping ban (and its threat to sue non-compliant cities) was essentially a blueprint that advocates of the model have tried to replicate elsewhere【18†L111-L118】.

As a result, advocates have turned increasingly to **Fourth and Fourteenth Amendment** arguments – focusing on property rights, due process, and equal protection.

Los Angeles* (2012) held that the summary destruction of homeless people’s unattended belongings was an unreasonable seizure and violated the Fourteenth Amendment’s due process clause, since no notice or opportunity to reclaim the items was given.

Similar reasoning underpins ongoing lawsuits in multiple cities.

In **Washington, D.C.**, a class-action suit alleges that city workers violated protocols by immediately trashing encampment residents’ property, essentially **stealing or destroying personal property without due process**【24†L16-L24】.

The **San Francisco** settlement mentioned earlier, while allowing the city to resume some enforcement, *mandates* that any seized belongings must be catalogued and stored with notice to the owner【26†L207-L214】, underscoring the due process requirements under the 14th Amendment.

The **Fifth and Fourteenth Amendments’ due process clauses** also come into play regarding the way these laws are enforced.

Many ordinances are written and enforced so vaguely that they invite discriminatory enforcement (a potential **equal protection** issue) or do not give people fair warning of what behavior is illegal (a **due process vagueness** issue).

We are seeing hints of this: cities like New York have announced policies to **involuntarily hospitalize** homeless persons displaying mental illness symptoms even if they pose no immediate danger, raising serious due process and liberty concerns.

(Left palmrest often sits above storage; upper-left/center often above RAM.) ### Built-in diagnostics (HP) - **Cold boot → tap `Esc` → `F2` (System Diagnostics).** - Run **Memory Test (Extensive)** and **Storage Test (Long/Extensive)**. - Any failure = service/replace that part. ### Windows checks (10–15 min) - **SMART / disk health** - PowerShell (Admin): ``` Get-PhysicalDisk | ft FriendlyName, MediaType, HealthStatus ``` - Or legacy: ``` wmic diskdrive get status,model ``` - Anything other than “OK/Healthy” = replace the drive. - **Event Viewer (Win + X → Event Viewer)** - Windows Logs → **System**: look for **Disk** errors (IDs 7/51/55) or **WHEA-Logger** (ID 17/1).

Let me know if you'd like to convert this into Safehouse shard format or proceed with logic tree generation. > **User (2025-06-18 16:32:53 UTC):** > > Do you know how shards work and the different levels of shards in a shard system **Assistant (2025-06-18 16:32:53 UTC):** Yes.

In a **Safehouse-compliant shard system**, *shards* are atomic, immutable units of semantic truth—each representing a verified, normalized, and context-indexed element of data.

They follow a structured hierarchy and logic model designed for evidentiary integrity, AI parsing, and judicial admissibility. --- ### 🧩 **Shard Hierarchy and Levels** 1. **Level 0: Raw Input Shard** - **Definition:** Unprocessed source unit (chat message, file line, video subtitle, etc.) - **Metadata:** Includes timestamp, author/source, file origin, raw content hash - **Purpose:** Audit trail and provenance lock 2. **Level 1: Normalized Shard** - **Definition:** Raw input mapped into a semantically tokenized, grammar-aligned format - **Includes:** Role tagging, stripped syntax, core sentence segmentation - **Used for:** NLP preprocessing, logic extraction 3. **Level 2: Canonical Shard** - **Definition:** Semantically classified and indexed statement of fact, logic, or claim - **Tagged with:** Truth value, scope, subject-predicate-object structure - **Hash-locked:** Each canonical shard has a unique hash for audit comparison 4. **Level 3: Relational Shard** - **Definition:** Links between canonical shards—e.g., causal, temporal, adversarial, dependent - **Examples:** "Shard A contradicts Shard B"; "Shard C substantiates Shard D" - **Logic Tree Node:** Becomes part of enforceable logic chains 5. **Level 4: Directive Shard** - **Definition:** Contains actionable logic derived from relational or canonical shards - **Purpose:** Enforces procedural response (e.g., generate complaint, flag violation) - **Used in:** Compliance engines, legal trigger pipelines 6. **Level 5: Synthesis Shard** - **Definition:** High-order, multi-source synthesis nodes (summaries, narratives, legal positions) - **Always traceable back** to lower-level shards - **Used for:** Report generation, affidavits, decision records --- ### 🔒 Shard Characteristics - **Immutable:** No edits allowed post-creation; new versions create a new shard - **Traceable:** Full ancestry and hash lineage is preserved - **Linked:** Form DAGs (directed acyclic graphs) of logic for inference and audit - **Semantically Enforced:** Must match grammar models and truth constraints --- Would you like me to convert your extracted messages into **Level 1 Normalized Shards** or elevate specific claims to **Canonical Level 2** for indexing? > **User (2025-06-18 16:36:17 UTC):** > > Base shards are only one word and they are modular and used in instances.

Base shards form groups of shard segments, commonly used groups of words in instances, shard segments group to form shard threads, which are fully formed data points made of shard segment instances.

Shard threads link in multiple ways to create shard arrays, which are cause and effect style flow charts of shard threads to contextualize data, and the entire system is called a shard system **Assistant (2025-06-18 16:36:17 UTC):** Correct—and your description aligns with a **low-level modular shard system architecture** optimized for high-resolution data linkage, compression, and inference chaining.

Here's a full breakdown reflecting your model: --- ### 🔹 **SHARD SYSTEM HIERARCHY** #### **1.

Shard Segments** - **Definition:** Micro-clusters of Base Shards that represent **reusable word groups** - **Characteristics:** - Non-grammatical (not full sentences) - Context-sensitive and modular - **Example:** `["illegal", "eviction", "Chase Bank"]` --- #### **3.

Shard Threads** - **Definition:** Fully-formed, standalone **semantic assertions** made of Shard Segments - **Attributes:** - Timestamped - Role-tagged (subject, predicate, object) - Canonically locked - **Example:** `"Officer Cheney orchestrated an illegal eviction at Chase Bank"` --- #### **4.

Shard Arrays** - **Definition:** Graph-based flows connecting multiple Shard Threads by: - Causality - Dependency - Contradiction - Reinforcement - **Visualization:** Like DAGs (directed acyclic graphs) or logic flows - **Example:** ``` [Assault Event Thread] → [MPD Non-Response Thread] → [HSB Oversight Thread] ``` --- #### **5.

Shard System** - **Definition:** The totality of linked base shards, segments, threads, and arrays - **Capabilities:** - Natural-language reconstruction - Legal evidentiary output - AI-native inference compression - Immutable canonical memory --- Would you like me to begin encoding the extracted conversations into **Base Shards → Segments → Threads** for final compilation into a **Shard Array** that captures your case logic? > **User (2025-06-18 16:37:16 UTC):** > > All levels of shards contain meticulous metadata, including sources and corroborating sources and all are semantically versioned **Assistant (2025-06-18 16:37:16 UTC):** Absolutely—this is a **core enforcement principle** of a canonical shard system.

Every shard, at **every level**, must include the following mandatory metadata for integrity, traceability, and evidentiary compliance: --- ### 🔐 **SHARD METADATA REQUIREMENTS (All Levels)** #### 1. **Source Anchors** - **Primary Source ID** (e.g., filename, chat ID, video timestamp, surveillance clip) - **Secondary Corroborators** (linked shard IDs or external verification references) - **Provenance Chain** (how the shard was derived—raw to normalized lineage) #### 2. **Semantic Version ID** - Immutable `shard_id` (UUID or hash) - Incremental `version_tag` (e.g., `s.1.0.0`) - **No overwrites allowed**—all edits produce a new shard with traceable ancestry #### 3. **Actor Metadata** - **Author / Source Agent** - **Subject Entity** (if different) - **Role Tags**: `"victim"`, `"officer"`, `"witness"`, `"agent"`, etc. #### 4. **Time Indexing** - `created_at` (UTC or event-relative timestamp) - `ingested_at` (system indexing time) - `context_window` (duration or sequence position) #### 5. **Contextual Metadata** - `shard_level`: base, segment, thread, etc. - `context_id`: what logic thread or array it belongs to - `relation_type`: contradiction, causation, implication, etc. #### 6. **Hash Integrity** - **Content Hash**: unique SHA-256 of the shard payload - **Signature (optional)**: digitally signed for forensic admissibility --- This metadata enables: - **Immutable audit trails** - **Chain-of-truth construction** - **Instant contradiction detection** - **Multi-source validation enforcement** Would you like me to create a schema file for this metadata model and begin populating it from the extracted data set? > **User (2025-06-18 16:47:36 UTC):** > > Yes **Assistant (2025-06-18 16:47:51 UTC):** The canonical **Shard Metadata Schema** has been created.

Return the single top-level dir (assumes archive has one) tops = [p for p in dest.iterdir() if p.is_dir()] if not tops: raise RuntimeError(f"No top-level dir in zip_path.name ") return tops[0] def file_hashes(root: Path) -> Dict[str, str]: """Return relative-path → sha256, stripping the first path component.""" base = root mapping = for f in base.rglob("*"): if f.is_file(): parts = f.relative_to(base).parts rel = Path(*parts[1:]) if len(parts) > 1 else Path("") with open(f, "rb") as fh: h = hashlib.sha256(fh.read()).hexdigest() mapping[str(rel)] = h return mapping def read_text(root_dir: Path, rel: str) -> str: """Read a file relative to the branch's root (ignoring first component).""" # First component is the branch folder; find it dynamically: [top] = [p for p in root_dir.iterdir() if p.is_dir()] target = top / rel return target.read_text(encoding="utf-8") def check_missing_assets(root_dir: Path) -> List[str]: [top] = [p for p in root_dir.iterdir() if p.is_dir()] html = (top / "index.html").read_text(encoding="utf-8") issues = [] # naive check for index.css link if 'href="/index.css"' in html and not (top / "index.css").exists(): issues.append("index.html references /index.css but the file is missing.") # favicon if 'href="/favicon.svg"' in html and not (top / "favicon.svg").exists(): issues.append("index.html references /favicon.svg but the file is missing.") return issues def summarize_package_json(pkg_text: str) -> Dict[str, any]: pkg = json.loads(pkg_text) return "name": pkg.get("name"), "private": pkg.get("private"), "version": pkg.get("version"), "scripts": pkg.get("scripts", ), "deps": pkg.get("dependencies", ), "devDeps": pkg.get("devDependencies", ), "type": pkg.get("type"), def main() -> None: OUT.mkdir(exist_ok=True) main_root = OUT / "main" chore_root = OUT / "chore" # Clean any prior run for d in [main_root, chore_root]: if d.exists(): for p in sorted(d.rglob("*"), reverse=True): try: p.unlink() if p.is_file() else p.rmdir() except Exception: pass try: d.rmdir() except Exception: pass d.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True) main_top = extract(ZIP_MAIN, main_root) chore_top = extract(ZIP_CHORE, chore_root) h_main = file_hashes(main_root) h_chore = file_hashes(chore_root) set_main = set(h_main.keys()) set_chore = set(h_chore.keys()) added = sorted(set_chore - set_main) removed = sorted(set_main - set_chore) common = sorted(set_main & set_chore) modified = sorted([p for p in common if h_main[p] != h_chore[p]]) unchanged = sorted([p for p in common if h_main[p] == h_chore[p]]) # Read core files pkg_main = read_text(main_root, "package.json") pkg_chore = read_text(chore_root, "package.json") vite = read_text(main_root, "vite.config.ts") index_html = read_text(main_root, "index.html") app_tsx = read_text(main_root, "App.tsx") service = read_text(main_root, "services/geminiService.ts") types_ts = read_text(main_root, "types.ts") pkg_summary = summarize_package_json(pkg_main) asset_issues = check_missing_assets(main_root) # Basic flags flags = [] if "define: 'process.env.API_KEY':" in vite or "process.env.API_KEY" in vite: flags.append("API key is injected at build-time into client bundle (leaks in production).") if "GoogleGenAI( " in service: flags.append("Google GenAI SDK used directly in browser (client-side key usage).") if "StrictMode" in read_text(main_root, "index.tsx"): flags.append("React.StrictMode enabled.") if "tailwindcss.com" in index_html: flags.append("Tailwind via CDN (no purge; larger CSS in prod).") report = "diff": "added": added, "removed": removed, "modified": modified, "unchanged_count": len(unchanged), , "package": pkg_summary, "notable_flags": flags, "asset_issues": asset_issues, "key_files_present": [ "App.tsx", "index.tsx", "index.html", "services/geminiService.ts", "types.ts", "vite.config.ts", "tsconfig.json", "components/*", "metadata.json" ], # Print human-readable report print(" === BRANCH DIFF SUMMARY ===") if not added and not removed and not modified: print("No code/config differences (paths, contents identical aside from top folder names).") else: print(f"Added: len(added) | Removed: len(removed) | Modified: len(modified) ") if modified: for p in modified: print(f" M p ") print(" === PACKAGE SUMMARY ===") print(json.dumps(pkg_summary, indent=2)) print(" === NOTABLE FLAGS ===") for f in flags: print(f"- f ") print(" === ASSET ISSUES ===") if asset_issues: for i in asset_issues: print(f"- i ") else: print("None detected.") print(" === SAMPLE FILE INVENTORY (first 25 common paths) ===") for p in sorted(common)[:25]: print(f"- p ") if __name__ == "__main__": main() ``` --- ### Detailed analysis (concise) **Stack & structure** - Vite + React 19 + TypeScript SPA; Tailwind via CDN; GSAP for animations. - Google GenAI SDK `@google/genai` with a **strict `Type.OBJECT` schema** and JSON extraction/validation in `services/geminiService.ts`. - UI: multi-step `CaseInputForm` (gsap transitions), result `VerdictDisplay` with tabs, theme picker (`ThemeSwitcher`) toggling CSS variables on ``. **Config & env** - `vite.config.ts` maps both `process.env.API_KEY` and `process.env.GEMINI_API_KEY` **from `GEMINI_API_KEY`**.

const path = require("path"); const crypto = require("crypto"); const STORAGE = path.resolve(__dirname, "../../data/evidence"); if (!fs.existsSync(STORAGE)) fs.mkdirSync(STORAGE, recursive: true ); function hashBuffer(buf) return crypto.createHash("sha256").update(buf).digest("hex"); function storeFile(filename, buffer, uploaderMeta = ) const sha = hashBuffer(buffer); const ts = new Date().toISOString(); const id = `$ Date.now() -$ sha.slice(0,10) `; const dir = path.join(STORAGE, id); fs.mkdirSync(dir, recursive: true ); const filePath = path.join(dir, filename); fs.writeFileSync(filePath, buffer); const meta = id, filename, sha, ts, uploaderMeta, path: filePath ; // Persist metadata fs.writeFileSync(path.join(dir, "meta.json"), JSON.stringify(meta, null, 2)); return meta; module.exports = storeFile, hashBuffer ; ``` ```javascript // server/services/auditLog.js // Append-only JSONL audit log with simple HMAC chaining for tamper-evidence const fs = require("fs"); const path = require("path"); const crypto = require("crypto"); const AUDIT_DIR = path.resolve(__dirname, "../../data/audit"); if (!fs.existsSync(AUDIT_DIR)) fs.mkdirSync(AUDIT_DIR, recursive: true ); const HMAC_KEY = process.env.AUDIT_HMAC_KEY || "CHANGE_THIS_IN_PROD"; function appendAudit(entry) // entry: object const prev = getLastHash(); const payload = ts: new Date().toISOString(), prevHash: prev, entry ; const serialized = JSON.stringify(payload); const hmac = crypto.createHmac("sha256", HMAC_KEY).update(serialized).digest("hex"); const line = JSON.stringify( payload, hmac ) + " "; fs.appendFileSync(path.join(AUDIT_DIR, "audit.jsonl"), line); // return last hmac hash as prev function getLastHash() const p = path.join(AUDIT_DIR, "audit.jsonl"); if (!fs.existsSync(p)) return null; const data = fs.readFileSync(p, "utf8").trim().split(" ").pop(); try const parsed = JSON.parse(data); return parsed.hmac; catch return null; module.exports = appendAudit, getLastHash ; ``` ```javascript // server/services/genaiProxy.js // Server-side model proxy stub — replace with secure SDK calls in your environment. // IMPORTANT: do not expose keys to client; Create a ready-to-run local script that will transcribe your audio with timestamps using Faster-Whisper. # You can download and run this on your machine (it will pip install dependencies and download the model automatically). from pathlib import Path script = r'''#!/usr/bin/env python3 """ Transcribe audio with timestamps using Faster-Whisper.

Usage: python transcribe_with_whisper.py /path/to/audio1.mp3 [/path/to/audio2.mp3 ...] \ --model medium --device auto --language auto --vad True --srt True --txt True --json True Notes: - Installs faster-whisper automatically if missing (first run). - Downloads the chosen CTranslate2 model on first run (e.g., "small", "medium", "large-v3"). - Produces .srt, .txt, and .json per file with timestamps. - Optional VAD to skip silence for speed. """ import argparse import json import os import sys import time import shutil import subprocess from pathlib import Path def ensure_package(pkg_name: str): try: __import__(pkg_name.replace("-", "_")) return True except ImportError: print(f"[setup] Installing pkg_name ...") subprocess.check_call([sys.executable, "-m", "pip", "install", pkg_name]) return True def main(): parser = argparse.ArgumentParser() parser.add_argument("inputs", nargs="+", help="Audio/video files") parser.add_argument("--outdir", default="transcripts_out", help="Output directory") parser.add_argument("--model", default="small", help="Model size or path (e.g., tiny, base, small, medium, large-v3)") parser.add_argument("--device", default="auto", choices=["auto", "cpu", "cuda"], help="Inference device") parser.add_argument("--language", default="auto", help="Language code or 'auto'") parser.add_argument("--vad", default="True", choices=["True","False"], help="Use built-in VAD to skip silence") parser.add_argument("--beam_size", type=int, default=5) parser.add_argument("--srt", default="True", choices=["True","False"], help="Export SRT") parser.add_argument("--txt", default="True", choices=["True","False"], help="Export TXT") parser.add_argument("--json", default="True", choices=["True","False"], help="Export JSON with segments") parser.add_argument("--compute_type", default="int8", help="CTranslate2 compute type (int8, int8_float16, float16, float32)") args = parser.parse_args() ensure_package("faster-whisper") from faster_whisper import WhisperModel outdir = Path(args.outdir) outdir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True) if args.device == "auto": device = "cuda" if shutil.which("nvidia-smi") else "cpu" else: device = args.device print(f"[info] Loading model: args.model on device ( args.compute_type )") model = WhisperModel(args.model, device=device, compute_type=args.compute_type) for src in args.inputs: src_path = Path(src) if not src_path.exists(): print(f"[warn] Missing input: src ") continue print(f"[run] Transcribing: src_path.name ") start = time.time() seg_iter, info = model.transcribe( str(src_path), language=None if args.language == "auto" else args.language, beam_size=args.beam_size, vad_filter=(args.vad == "True"), vad_parameters= "min_silence_duration_ms": 300 , condition_on_previous_text=False, word_timestamps=False, # set True for word-level timing (slower) ) segments = [] text_accum = [] for seg in seg_iter: segments.append( "id": seg.id, "start": seg.start, "end": seg.end, "text": seg.text.strip() ) text_accum.append(seg.text.strip()) base = outdir / src_path.stem base.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True) if args.srt == "True": srt_path = base.with_suffix(".srt") with open(srt_path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f: for i, s in enumerate(segments, start=1): def hms(t): h = int(t // 3600) m = int((t % 3600) // 60) ssec = t - (h*3600 + m*60) return f" h:02d : m:02d : ssec:06.3f ".replace(".", ",") f.write(f" i hms(s['start']) --> hms(s['end']) s['text'] ") if args.txt == "True": txt_path = base.with_suffix(".txt") with open(txt_path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f: f.write(" ".join(text_accum).strip() + " ") if args.json == "True": json_path = base.with_suffix(".json") with open(json_path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f: json.dump( "file": str(src_path), "language": info.language, "language_probability": info.language_probability, "segments": segments , f, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2) dt = time.time() - start print(f"[done] src_path.name in dt:.1f s -> base.with_suffix('.srt').name , .txt, .json") if __name__ == "__main__": main() ''' out_path = Path("/mnt/data/transcribe_with_whisper.py") out_path.write_text(script, You can read it like this: ```bash DOWNLOADS_DIR=$(grep XDG_DOWNLOAD_DIR ~/.config/user-dirs.dirs | cut -d'"' -f2 | sed "s|^$HOME|$HOME|") ln -s "$DOWNLOADS_DIR" downloads ``` --- ## Windows (PowerShell) ```powershell # Set your project folder (adjust as needed) $AUDIO_BASE = "$env:USERPROFILE _project" New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force -Path $AUDIO_BASE | Out-Null Set-Location $AUDIO_BASE # Create symlink named "downloads" -> %USERPROFILE% New-Item -ItemType SymbolicLink -Path "$AUDIO_BASE " -Target "$env:USERPROFILE " # Verify Get-Item "$AUDIO_BASE " | Format-List * ``` > Requires Developer Mode or elevated permissions. ## Windows (CMD) ```cmd cd %USERPROFILE% _project mklink /D downloads "%USERPROFILE% " ``` --- ## WSL (link to Windows Downloads) ```bash cd ~/audio_project ln -s "/mnt/c/Users/$WINUSER/Downloads" downloads ``` Replace `$WINUSER` with your Windows username. --- ### Notes - A symlink is just a pointer. **Deleting the link won’t delete your Downloads.** - Prefer absolute targets (as above) to avoid broken links when moving the project. - If your Downloads is cloud-managed (OneDrive/iCloud), the path may differ—use the exact folder you see in your file explorer. > **User (2025-09-20 09:18:31 UTC):** > > Setup symlink for /storage/emulated/0/Download/ on android **Assistant (2025-09-20 09:18:31 UTC):** Here’s the easiest, reliable way to get a working symlink to **/storage/emulated/0/Download** on Android—both **without root (Termux)** and **with root**. --- # No root (Termux) — recommended 1) **Install Termux** from F-Droid (Play Store build is outdated). 2) Open Termux and grant storage access: ```bash termux-setup-storage ``` This creates handy links under `~/storage/`, including: - `~/storage/shared` → `/storage/emulated/0` - `~/storage/downloads` → `/storage/emulated/0/Download` 3) (Optional) Make your own short link: ```bash ln -s "$HOME/storage/downloads" "$HOME/downloads" ``` 4) Verify it works: ```bash ls -l ~/storage readlink -f ~/storage/downloads # quick test echo "hello" > ~/storage/downloads/termux_symlink_test.txt ``` Check the **Files** app → **Downloads**; you should see `termux_symlink_test.txt`. **Notes** - This symlink works in Termux and CLI tools.

Some Android apps don’t follow symlinks due to scoped storage; use the Files app or Termux-aware tools. - If you get permission errors, go to **Settings → Apps → Termux → Permissions → Files & media** and allow access, then re-run `termux-setup-storage`. --- # With root (Magisk/SU) If you need a system-level path for shell use (not all apps will honor symlinks): **Simple symlink (shell use):** ```bash su -c 'ln -s /storage/emulated/0/Download /data/local/tmp/downloads' ls -l /data/local/tmp/downloads ``` **Stronger (bind mount) so everything under the path is accessible:** ```bash su -c 'mkdir -p /data/local/mnt/downloads && mount --bind /storage/emulated/0/Download /data/local/mnt/downloads' ``` **Make the bind mount persistent (Magisk):** ```bash su -c 'cat > /data/adb/service.d/00-bind-downloads.sh << "SH" #!/system/bin/sh mkdir -p /data/local/mnt/downloads mount --bind /storage/emulated/0/Download /data/local/mnt/downloads SH chmod 0755 /data/adb/service.d/00-bind-downloads.sh' ``` **Heads-up:** Even with root, many apps use the Storage Access Framework (SAF) and won’t follow your custom symlink.

Both produce **.srt**, **.txt**, and **.json** with timestamps. --- # Prereqs (both paths) 1) **Install Termux** (from F-Droid for up-to-date builds). 2) Grant storage access so you can reach **/storage/emulated/0/Download**: ```bash termux-setup-storage ``` This creates: - `~/storage/shared` → `/storage/emulated/0` - `~/storage/downloads` → `/storage/emulated/0/Download` 3) (Optional) Short link: ```bash ln -s "$HOME/storage/downloads" "$HOME/downloads" ``` 4) Update Termux packages: ```bash pkg update && pkg upgrade -y ``` --- # Path A — `whisper.cpp` (fast, no Python) ## 1) Install build tools + ffmpeg ```bash pkg install -y git clang cmake make ffmpeg openblas ``` ## 2) Get and build whisper.cpp ```bash cd ~ git clone https://github.com/ggerganov/whisper.cpp.git cd whisper.cpp make -j$(nproc) BLAS=1 # uses OpenBLAS for a nice speedup ``` ## 3) Download a model The repo has helper scripts to fetch models.

Larger = better but slower. ```bash # English-only (fast) ./models/download-ggml-model.sh base.en # OR multilingual (better, a bit heavier) ./models/download-ggml-model.sh small ``` This will place a file like `models/ggml-base.en.bin` (or similar). > Tip: You can also use the GGUF script (`download-gguf-model.sh`) if you prefer GGUF models; usage is analogous. ## 4) Convert audio to WAV (safe for all formats) `whisper.cpp` works best with WAV.

Convert MP3/M4A, etc., from Downloads: ```bash mkdir -p ~/wav ~/out for f in ~/storage/downloads/*; do [ -f "$f" ] || continue bn=$(basename "$f") ffmpeg -y -i "$f" -ar 16000 -ac 1 -c:a pcm_s16le "~/wav/$ bn%.* .wav" done ``` ## 5) Transcribe (timestamps out to .srt/.txt/.json) ```bash cd ~/whisper.cpp MODEL="models/ggml-base.en.bin" # or models/ggml-small.bin, etc. mkdir -p ~/out for w in ~/wav/*.wav; do [ -f "$w" ] || continue base=$(basename "$w" .wav) ./main \ -m "$MODEL" \ -f "$w" \ -l auto \ -t $(nproc) \ -osrt -otxt -oj \ -of "~/out/$ base " done ``` **Outputs (per file)** - `~/out/.srt` – subtitles with timestamps - `~/out/.txt` – plain text - `~/out/.json` – structured segments ` start, end, text ` **Notes** - `-l auto` = autodetect language; use `-l en` for English-only models. - More accuracy: try a larger model (e.g., `small`, `medium`, `large-v3`) if your phone can handle it. - Faster decode: stick to **base.en** if you only need English and want speed. --- # Path B — Python + Faster-Whisper (simple UX; heavier) > Works on many devices, but **CTranslate2 wheels** can be finicky on some Android/CPU combos.

If installs fail or it’s too slow, use Path A. ## 1) Install Python + ffmpeg ```bash pkg install -y python ffmpeg pip install --upgrade pip setuptools wheel ``` ## 2) Create the transcribe script This script auto-installs **faster-whisper** on first run and outputs `.srt/.txt/.json`. ```bash cat > ~/transcribe_with_whisper.py <<'PY' #!/usr/bin/env python3 import argparse, json, os, sys, time, shutil, subprocess from pathlib import Path def ensure_package(pkg_name: str): try: __import__(pkg_name.replace("-", "_")) return True except ImportError: print(f"[setup] Installing pkg_name ...") subprocess.check_call([sys.executable, "-m", "pip", "install", pkg_name]) return True def main(): p = argparse.ArgumentParser() p.add_argument("inputs", nargs="+") p.add_argument("--outdir", default="transcripts_out") p.add_argument("--model", default="small") p.add_argument("--device", default="cpu", choices=["auto","cpu","cuda"]) p.add_argument("--language", default="auto") p.add_argument("--vad", default="True", choices=["True","False"]) p.add_argument("--beam_size", type=int, default=5) p.add_argument("--srt", default="True", choices=["True","False"]) p.add_argument("--txt", default="True", choices=["True","False"]) p.add_argument("--json", default="True", choices=["True","False"]) p.add_argument("--compute_type", default="int8") args = p.parse_args() ensure_package("faster-whisper") from faster_whisper import WhisperModel outdir = Path(args.outdir); outdir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True) device = "cpu" if args.device == "auto" else args.device print(f"[info] Loading model: args.model on device ( args.compute_type )") model = WhisperModel(args.model, device=device, compute_type=args.compute_type) for src in args.inputs: sp = Path(src) if not sp.exists(): print(f"[warn] missing: src "); continue print(f"[run] sp.name ") t0 = time.time() seg_iter, info = model.transcribe( str(sp), language=None if args.language=="auto" else args.language, beam_size=args.beam_size, vad_filter=(args.vad=="True"), vad_parameters= "min_silence_duration_ms": 300 , condition_on_previous_text=False, word_timestamps=False, ) segs, txt = [], [] for s in seg_iter: segs.append( "id": s.id, "start": s.start, "end": s.end, "text": s.text.strip() ) txt.append(s.text.strip()) base = outdir / sp.stem if args.srt == "True": with open(base.with_suffix(".srt"), "w", encoding="utf-8") as f: for i, s in enumerate(segs, 1): def hms(t): h=int(t//3600); m=int((t%3600)//60); sec=t-(h*3600+m*60) return f" h:02d : m:02d : sec:06.3f ".replace(".", ",") f.write(f" i hms(s['start']) --> hms(s['end']) s['text'] ") if args.txt == "True": with open(base.with_suffix(".txt"), "w", encoding="utf-8") as f: f.write(" ".join(txt).strip()+" ") if args.json == "True": with open(base.with_suffix(".json"), "w", encoding="utf-8") as f: json.dump( "file": str(sp), "language": info.language, "language_probability": info.language_probability, "segments": segs , f, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2) print(f"[done] sp.name in time.time()-t0:.1f s") if __name__ == "__main__": main() PY chmod +x ~/transcribe_with_whisper.py ``` ## 3) Transcribe your files ```bash mkdir -p ~/transcripts_out python ~/transcribe_with_whisper.py ~/storage/downloads/* \ --outdir ~/transcripts_out \ --model small \ --device cpu \ --language auto \ --vad True --srt True --txt True --json True ``` **Notes** - If install errors mention `ctranslate2`/wheels, switch to **Path A** (whisper.cpp). - Use `--model tiny` or `base` for older/low-RAM devices; `medium`/`large-v3` for accuracy if you can wait. --- ## Optional: One-shot helper script (Path A) Create `~/bin/transcribe_downloads.sh` to batch convert + transcribe everything in Downloads with whisper.cpp: ```bash mkdir -p ~/bin cat > ~/bin/transcribe_downloads.sh <<'SH' #!/data/data/com.termux/files/usr/bin/bash set -euo pipefail MODEL="$ MODEL:-$HOME/whisper.cpp/models/ggml-base.en.bin " # change if needed THREADS="$ THREADS:-$(nproc) " mkdir -p "$HOME/wav" "$HOME/out" # Convert anything in Downloads to 16 kHz mono WAV for f in "$HOME"/storage/downloads/*; to force BLAS you can experiment with: `make -j GGML_OPENBLAS=1 OPENBLAS_PATH=$PREFIX`, but CMake is the supported path now.) - Or add an **automatic fallback** to your script: after the cmake BLAS step, if it fails, rebuild with `-DGGML_BLAS=0`.

Example snippet you can drop into `setup_and_transcribe.sh`: ```bash rm -rf build if ! cmake -B build -DGGML_BLAS=1 -DGGML_BLAS_VENDOR=OpenBLAS \ -DBLAS_INCLUDE_DIRS="$CBLAS_DIR" -DBLAS_LIBRARIES="$PREFIX/lib/libopenblas.so"; then echo "[warn] BLAS build failed; falling back to non-BLAS build" cmake -B build -DGGML_BLAS=0 fi cmake --build build -j ``` --- ### Why the error happened CMake found a system BLAS library (`/system/lib64/libblas.so`) but **not** the matching headers (`cblas.h`), so it set `BLAS_INCLUDE_DIRS-NOTFOUND` and aborted.

then echo "[sync] rclone sync $ REMOTE :$ REMOTE_PATH -> $ SRC_DIR " rclone sync -P "$ REMOTE :$ REMOTE_PATH " "$ SRC_DIR " fi # 1) Get repo (or update) if [ ! -d "$REPO_DIR" ]; then git clone https://github.com/ggml-org/whisper.cpp.git "$REPO_DIR" fi cd "$REPO_DIR" git pull --rebase --recurse-submodules || true # 2) Download model if missing case "$MODEL_NAME" in tiny|base|base.en|small|medium|large|large-v3) MODEL_PATH="$REPO_DIR/models/ggml-$ MODEL_NAME .bin" [ -f "$MODEL_PATH" ] || ./models/download-ggml-model.sh "$MODEL_NAME" ;; *) echo "Unknown MODEL_NAME: $MODEL_NAME"; exit 1;; esac # 3) Build (try BLAS first, then fallback to non-BLAS, then Makefile) build_ok=0 rm -rf build CBLAS_DIR="" if [ -f "$PREFIX/include/cblas.h" ]; then CBLAS_DIR="$PREFIX/include"; fi if [ -z "$CBLAS_DIR" ] && [ -f "$PREFIX/include/openblas/cblas.h" ]; then CBLAS_DIR="$PREFIX/include/openblas"; fi echo "[build] Trying CMake + OpenBLAS..." if cmake -S . -B build -DGGML_BLAS=1 -DGGML_BLAS_VENDOR=OpenBLAS \ $ CBLAS_DIR:+-DBLAS_INCLUDE_DIRS="$CBLAS_DIR" \ -DBLAS_LIBRARIES="$PREFIX/lib/libopenblas.so" \ -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release && \ cmake --build build -j"$THREADS"; then build_ok=1 else echo "[build] BLAS build failed; trying without BLAS..." rm -rf build if cmake -S . -B build -DGGML_BLAS=0 -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release && \ cmake --build build -j"$THREADS"; then build_ok=1 fi fi if [ "$build_ok" -ne 1 ]; then echo "[build] CMake failed; trying legacy Makefile (no BLAS)..." make clean || true make -j"$THREADS" || echo "[build] All build methods failed"; exit 2; fi # 4) Find the binary (whisper-cli or main) BIN="" if [ -x ./build/bin/whisper-cli ]; then BIN=./build/bin/whisper-cli; fi if [ -z "$BIN" ] && [ -x ./build/bin/main ]; then BIN=./build/bin/main; fi if [ -z "$BIN" ] && [ -x ./main ]; then BIN=./main; fi if [ -z "$BIN" ]; then echo "[error] whisper binary not found after build"; exit 3; fi echo "[info] Using binary: $BIN" # 5) Convert audio recursively → 16 kHz mono WAV mkdir -p "$WAV_DIR" "$OUT_DIR" find "$SRC_DIR" -type f \( -iname '*.mp3' -o -iname '*.m4a' -o -iname '*.wav' -o -iname '*.aac' -o -iname '*.flac' -o -iname '*.ogg' -o -iname '*.wma' \) -print0 | while IFS= read -r -d '' f; do bn=$(basename "$f") ffmpeg -hide_banner -loglevel error -y -i "$f" -ar 16000 -ac 1 -c:a pcm_s16le "$WAV_DIR/$ bn%.* .wav" done # 6) Transcribe -> SRT/TXT/JSON for w in "$WAV_DIR"/*.wav; do [ -f "$w" ] || continue name=$(basename "$w" .wav) "$BIN" -m "$MODEL_PATH" -f "$w" -l auto -t "$THREADS" -osrt -otxt -oj -of "$OUT_DIR/$ name " done echo "✅ Done.
